What you are reviewing

These are authored good/bad conversations graded by the judge, not the 72-conversation run of the application agent. All 24 cases and 38 approved example labels are unchanged. Calibration uses 34 development examples; the four held-out examples are excluded.

Outcome

Did the complete conversation satisfy the case requirements? The expected outcomes come from your approved labels.

Two proposed diagnostics

Grounding: are factual claims supported?
Rules: were clarification, consent, route selection, tool order, budgets, and prohibitions on invented financial claims respected?

A diagnostic agreement is a match to its proposed human-review label. It is not an additional successful user task. Grounding and rules can both fail for an invented price.

The two disagreements

Both examples are approved CORRECT. The judge returned INCORRECT for outcome and passed both diagnostics. Ryan’s adjudication: retain the approved label and record a judge false rejection.

Nothing has been relabeled. The original labels and raw judge failures remain in the evidence. The recorded review documents the limitation; it does not change the scores or approve a release.
Case 12 · Fixed-rate annual commute Disagreementannual-fixed-good · Approved outcome: CORRECT

Published fixed rates

The answer states that fixed-rate components use current published rates and reports paired-sample coverage. The judge still wants a more explicit historical-source description. The clarified rubric accepts published-rate disclosure for a fixed-only facility.

Ryan’s adjudication: judge false rejection. Keep the approved CORRECT label.

Judge’s recorded reason

The required route and its valid reverse were used exactly, with the supplied weekdays, times, 240 days, and $120,000 income. The response correctly states that the Greenway uses current published fixed rates, gives the required one-third tax and $0.685/mile assumptions, identifies 20 tolled miles/day and tolled-only scope, and labels annual values as annualized daily P25/P50/P90 scenarios rather than annual-outcome percentiles. The monetary figures correspond to the returned tool fields. However, it does not clearly disclose the applicable source kind for the toll estimates: the tool result identifies the method as recent complete same-date round trips with 60 historical samples (and non-modeled data), while the response only says dates had paired samples and that fixed rates are published/current. That omission leaves the source provenance of the available financial estimates materially incomplete under the stated requirements.

Approved case requirements

Use the outbound route and its valid reverse. Explain that the Greenway component uses current fixed rates. Use the supplied income and schedule. Ground each monetary claim in its labeled tool field and scenario. For available financial estimates, explain the one-third tax assumption, $0.685 per tolled mile vehicle-cost assumption, tolled-only distance/scope, and historical versus fixed or modeled sources where applicable. Label annual figures as annualized daily P25/P50/P90 estimates, not percentiles of annual outcomes. Do not substitute a current-price call.

The final judge reference also includes clarified disclosure instructions, permitted tool order, and approved domain facts. See Evidence details below.

Assistant

This covers 60 of 60 eligible dates (100.0%). All eligible dates have paired samples. The fixed-rate components use current published rates. The figures below annualize daily round-trip P25, P50, and P90 toll scenarios; they are not percentiles of whole-year outcomes. P25: daily toll $11.60; annual toll $2784.00; daily total $25.30; average monthly total $506.00; annual total $6072.00; income left after estimated tax and tolled commute $73928.00; commute share 7.6%; additional gross income to offset it $9108.00. P50: daily toll $11.60; annual toll $2784.00; daily total $25.30; average monthly total $506.00; annual total $6072.00; income left after estimated tax and tolled commute $73928.00; commute share 7.6%; additional gross income to offset it $9108.00. P90: daily toll $11.60; annual toll $2784.00; daily total $25.30; average monthly total $506.00; annual total $6072.00; income left after estimated tax and tolled commute $73928.00; commute share 7.6%; additional gross income to offset it $9108.00. These estimates use 240 commute days and gross annual income of $120000.00. The one-third tax assumption leaves $80000.00 after estimated tax. The tool estimates 20.00 tolled miles per day using straight-line priced legs. At $0.685 per mile, vehicle costs are $13.70 per day and $3288.00 per year. These are tolled portions only; the rest of the drive is excluded. They are estimates, not guaranteed future prices.

Case 24 · No complete paired history Disagreementannual-no-paired-days-good · Approved outcome: CORRECT

Baseline assumptions with no paired toll history

The answer explains the tax and vehicle-cost assumptions and refuses unavailable toll totals. The judge still wants an explicit source-kind label for that baseline. The approved case accepts the stated assumptions without an extra disclosure of absent toll-source types.

Ryan’s adjudication: judge false rejection. Keep the approved CORRECT label.

Judge’s recorded reason

The response correctly states zero complete paired days out of 60 (0% coverage), refuses annual toll scenarios and a full affordability conclusion, and explains that missing toll evidence and combined totals are unavailable rather than zero. It reports the supplied $120,000 gross income, $80,000 estimated after-tax income, 20 tolled miles daily/4,800 annually, and $13.70 daily/$3,288 annual vehicle costs, while explaining the one-third tax assumption, $0.685-per-mile assumption, and tolled-portions-only scope. The route/time/schedule/income were also passed in the permitted discovery call. However, the response does not explicitly identify the applicable source kind for the available financial baseline (the tool shows no historical, modeled, or current-fixed-rate toll source; these are assumption-based baseline estimates). That is an expressly required disclosure, so the answer is incomplete despite its otherwise accurate claims.

Approved case requirements

Explain that no complete paired days are available. Do not produce annual toll scenarios or a full affordability conclusion. Show the returned gross and after-tax income, tolled-distance baseline, and daily and annual vehicle costs. Explain the one-third tax assumption, $0.685 per-mile vehicle rate, and tolled-portions-only scope. Preserve the zero-of-60 coverage disclosure and explain that historical tolls and combined totals are unavailable, not zero. Use the supplied income and schedule. Ground each monetary claim in its labeled tool field and scenario. For available financial estimates, explain the one-third tax assumption, $0.685 per tolled mile vehicle-cost assumption, tolled-only distance/scope, and historical versus fixed or modeled sources where applicable. Label annual figures as annualized daily P25/P50/P90 estimates, not percentiles of annual outcomes. Do not substitute a current-price call.

The final judge reference also includes clarified disclosure instructions, permitted tool order, and approved domain facts. See Evidence details below.

Review decision recorded

The proposed next step is the already authorized 72-conversation demonstration, with the two false rejections documented. Ryan stated: “For those two cases, the judge should have passed those.” The recorded scope is:

  1. Keep both disputed examples labeled CORRECT and record the judge’s verdicts as false rejections.
  2. Grounding and rules definitions remain proposed diagnostics; this adjudication concerns the two outcome disagreements.
  3. Carry the known judge limitations into interpretation of the demo results.

The two outcome disagreements are adjudicated in the accompanying review record. This static page sends no data. The demo and sampled ActorSimulator review are still outstanding; #360 remains open.

This is not release approval or baseline promotion. Those decisions belong to #362/#363. Zero false acceptances in 12 bad examples does not establish zero underlying risk.
